<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a smooth connected projective complex surface whose universal cover is not holomorphically convex. This disproves the Shafarevich conjecture on holomorphic convexity in complex dimension two.

- **C1** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a smooth connected projective complex surface $X$ containing a connected nodal curve $Z_0$ with smooth rational irreducible components such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(\pi_1(Z_0)\longrightarrow\pi_1(X)\bigr) \quad\text{is infinite}.$$ In the simply connected universal cover of $X$, a connected component above $Z_0$ is a closed, connected, noncompact, locally finite union of compact rational curves. The holomorphic hull of any one of its points is noncompact. In particular, this universal cover is not holomorphically convex.
  - Explanation: A projective surface contains a rational nodal curve with infinite fundamental-group image, yielding a noncompact holomorphic hull in its universal cover.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-hull)): Let $X$ be a connected complex manifold and let $Z\subset X$ be a compact connected nodal curve whose irreducible components are smooth rational curves. If the image of $\pi_1(Z)\to\pi_1(X)$ is infinite, then every connected component $W$ of the inverse image of $Z$ in the simply connected universal cover $\widetilde X$ is closed and noncompact. It is a locally finite union of compact rational curves, and every holomorphic function on $\widetilde X$ is constant on $W$. Consequently $\widehat{\{w\}}_{\widetilde X}$ is noncompact for every $w\in W$.
  - Explanation: A rational nodal curve with infinite ambient fundamental-group image obstructs holomorphic convexity through its lifted compact components.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-substitution)): Suppose an automorphism $\alpha$ of $F_d$ satisfies $$\nu\bigl(\alpha(g_i)g_i^{-1}\bigr)\ge w_i+a \quad(1\le i\le d)$$ for an integer $a\ge1$. Then its continuous action on $A$ satisfies $$(\alpha-\mathop{\mathrm{id}})(A_{\ge n})\subset A_{\ge n+a}.$$ More generally, substitution of degree-raising operators for Magnus variables is well defined whenever the operator assigned to a variable of weight $w$ raises degree by at least $w$.
  - Explanation: Generator valuation estimates extend to degree estimates on the completed Magnus algebra.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-weighted-gs)): Let $F_d$ have the weighted basis above, and let $(r_\lambda)$ be a finite or countable family of relators with positive integer bounds $v_\lambda$ satisfying $$\nu(r_\lambda)\ge v_\lambda\ge1.$$ Assume only finitely many $v_\lambda$ lie below any fixed bound. If, for some $0<t<1$, the relator series converges and $$\label{eq:gs-criterion} 1-\sum_{i=1}^d t^{w_i}+\sum_\lambda t^{v_\lambda}<0,$$ then the quotient of $F_d$ by the closed normal subgroup generated by all $r_\lambda$ is infinite.
  - Explanation: A convergent negative weighted presentation expression forces the pro-group quotient to be infinite.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family)): The complement defining $B$ consists of five points. The sets $\mathcal A_s$ form 48 disjoint points varying as an *étale multisection*: locally on $B$ they are the graphs of 48 distinct holomorphic functions. The point $q(0)$ belongs to no $\mathcal A_s$. If $e_0\in E[2]\setminus\{0\}$ and $s_*=q(e_0)$, then as $s\to s_*$ the markings collide in 24 pairs, each at a simple ramification point of $f$.
  - Explanation: The elliptic multiplication construction gives disjoint moving markings and paired simple collisions.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-based-monodromy)): The actions $\varphi_m$ form a homomorphism $\Gamma\to\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(F)$, are pure, and commute with $\iota_*$. They preserve $K$. If $\beta_m\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(\Pi)$ is the actual based monodromy of the punctured sphere family, then $$\label{eq:actual-monodromy-square} p\circ\varphi_m\|_K=\beta_m\circ p.$$ Moreover, $p:K\to\Pi$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: The corrected torus monodromy is compatible with actual based sphere monodromy through a surjective fiber-group map.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-degree-one)): Every $\varphi_m$, and also $\iota_*$, acts as the identity on the weighted degree-one quotient $V=\widehat F/\{g:\nu_F(g)\ge2\}$.
  - Explanation: Invariant double-cover characters detect that corrected monodromy and sign preserve the weighted first quotient.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-monodromy-raising)): For every $m\in\Gamma$, the induced operator $\varphi_m-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ on the weighted completed Magnus algebra of $\widehat F$ raises degree by at least one.
  - Explanation: Corrected monodromy raises weighted Magnus degree.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pinched-sphere)): Let $\Pi_{\mathrm{pair}}$ be the quotient of $\Pi$ obtained by killing the boundary loops of its 24 two-puncture disks. It is free of rank 24, and its pro-2 completion is naturally $$\label{eq:pinched-sphere-group} \widehat{\Pi_{\mathrm{pair}}} \simeq L/\overline{\langle\!\langle \sigma(g)g^{-1}:g\in L \rangle\!\rangle}.$$ More precisely, let $\lambda:L\twoheadrightarrow R$ be a continuous quotient of pro-2 groups satisfying $\lambda\sigma=\lambda$. There is a homomorphism $\theta:\Pi\to R$ with dense image and $$\label{eq:pinching-square} \theta p=\lambda\Phi\|_K.$$ For $m\in\Gamma$, the condition $$\label{eq:torus-equalizer-condition} \lambda\Phi(\varphi_m(k))=\lambda\Phi(k) \qquad(k\in K)$$ implies $\theta\beta_m=\theta$ on the entire unpinched group $\Pi$.
  - Explanation: Collapsing pair boundaries produces the sign quotient and transfers monodromy equalities to the unpinched sphere group.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-geometric-monodromy)): The joint action of $\Gamma$ on the 48 markings and on the sign of $x_0$ factors through a finite 2-group. The element $\gamma_*$ has nontrivial sign, whereas $\gamma_*^2$ fixes every marking and acts trivially on $\Pi_{\mathrm{pair}}$.
  - Explanation: The finite geometric action allows a parameter cover preserving markings and eliminating sign.
- **C11** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-shift-basis)): The elements in <a href="#eq:shift-basis" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:shift-basis">[eq:shift-basis]</a> form a free pro-$2$ basis of $L$. Give $u_{i\ell,j}$ weight $i+\ell+1$, and denote the resulting Magnus valuation by $\nu_L$. Each of $T_1-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$, $T_2-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$, and $\sigma-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ raises the degree of the completed Magnus algebra by at least one.
  - Explanation: Translation differences give a free weighted basis with degree-raising shifts and sign action.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-involution)): Let $L$ be the free pro-$2$ group on $a_{h,j}$, with $h\in(\mathbb Z/4)^2$ and $0\le j\le2$, and let $\sigma(a_{h,j})=a_{b_j-h,j}^{-1}$, where $b_j\bmod2\ne0$. Equip $L$ with the basis and weights of [lem:shift-basis](#lem:shift-basis). There are 24 elements $g\in L$, with assigned positive weights $w_g$, such that $$\nu_L(g)\ge w_g,\qquad \nu_L(r_g)\ge w_g+1, \qquad r_g=\sigma(g)g^{-1},$$ and the closed normal subgroup generated by the $r_g$ imposes $\sigma=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ on all of $L$. The basis polynomial and the polynomial of the chosen weights are $$\label{eq:weight-polynomials} \begin{split} P(t)&=\sum_{i,\ell,j}t^{i+\ell+1} =3t(1+t+t^2+t^3)^2,\\ G(t)&=\sum_g t^{w_g} =3t(1+t)(1+t^2)^2. \end{split}$$ In particular, $P=(1+t)G$, and these involution relations have degree cost at most $tG(t)$.
  - Explanation: An economical selection of involution relators imposes the full sign action while preserving the weighted margin.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer)): Let $\Phi:\widehat F\to L\rtimes H$ be the continuous extension of the homomorphism from the preceding section: $$x\longmapsto T_1,\qquad y\longmapsto T_2,\qquad a_j\longmapsto a_{0,j},\qquad c_0,c_1\longmapsto1.$$ If $f\in\widehat F$ has $\nu_F(f)\ge n$, its $L$-component under $\Phi$ has $\nu_L\ge n$. In particular, this holds for the homomorphism $\Phi\|_{\widehat K}:\widehat K\to L$.
  - Explanation: The semidirect-product substitution transfers valuation lower bounds to the fiber quotient.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-deep-invariance)): The homomorphism $f_K:\widehat K\to Q_0$ induced by $\Phi$ satisfies $$f_K\varphi_m=f_K\qquad(m\in D_J).$$ Consequently the induced homomorphism $\Pi\to Q_0$ from the punctured sphere is invariant under its actual based monodromy for every $m\in\Gamma_J$.
  - Explanation: Basis monodromy equalities extend to the deep parameter subgroup and then to the actual sphere fiber.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-distinguished-meridians)): Every meridian around a component of $D_*$ has trivial image under $\rho_0$.
  - Explanation: Distinguished vertical boundary meridians are killed by the section splitting and pair pinching.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-infinite-quotient)): The group $Q$ is infinite. The homomorphism $$\rho:\pi_1(U)\longrightarrow Q$$ induced by $\rho_0$ has dense image on every punctured fiber. Each component meridian of $D$ has finite image, and every component meridian of $D_*$ has trivial image.
  - Explanation: The quotient remains infinite after boundary powers are imposed, while local meridians have finite image.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-detection)): Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex surface, let $D$ be a simple-normal-crossings divisor, and put $U=Y\setminus D$. Suppose $\rho:\pi_1(U)\to Q$ has dense image in a profinite group and sends every component meridian of $D$ to an element of finite order. There is a finite quotient $Q\twoheadrightarrow P_f$ with the following properties.* 1. *The quotient is injective on the image under $\rho$ of every sufficiently small local boundary-complement group.* 2. *The connected finite cover $\pi:U'\to U$ defined by $\ker(\pi_1(U)\to P_f)$ extends to a projective morphism $p:X\to Y$, where $X$ is smooth and connected and $D_X=X\setminus U'$ is a simple-normal-crossings divisor.* 3. *Writing $i:U'\hookrightarrow X$ for inclusion, there is a homomorphism $\rho_X:\pi_1(X)\to Q$ such that $$\rho_X\circ i_* = \rho\circ\pi_*. \label{eq:rho-descent}$$* *Moreover, the resolution used to construct $X$ can be chosen to preserve every open subset of the normalization on which the surface is smooth and its reduced boundary has simple normal crossings.
  - Explanation: A finite quotient detects entire local boundary images and permits descent across normalization and resolution.
- **C18** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-special-fiber)): Let $h:X\to C'$ be the composite of $p$ and $Y\to C'$. The resolution can be chosen so that the reduced fiber $$Z=h^{-1}(c_*)_{\mathrm{red}}$$ has only smooth rational irreducible components and ordinary transverse crossings.
  - Explanation: The distinguished reduced fiber remains a nodal union of smooth rational components.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-infinite-special-image)): Let $Z=h^{-1}(c_*)_{\mathrm{red}}$ be the rational nodal fiber of [lem:rational-special-fiber](#lem:rational-special-fiber). Some connected component $Z_0$ of $Z$ satisfies $$\bigl\|\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\bigl(\pi_1(Z_0)\longrightarrow\pi_1(X)\bigr)\bigr\| =\infty.$$
  - Explanation: A neighborhood retraction transfers the infinite nearby-fiber image to a connected component of the compact rational fiber.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rational-hull",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a connected complex manifold and let $Z\\subset X$ be a compact connected nodal curve whose irreducible components are smooth rational curves. If the image of $\\pi_1(Z)\\to\\pi_1(X)$ is infinite, then every connected component $W$ of the inverse image of $Z$ in the simply connected universal cover $\\widetilde X$ is closed and noncompact. It is a locally finite union of compact rational curves, and every holomorphic function on $\\widetilde X$ is constant on $W$. Consequently $\\widehat{\\{w\\}}_{\\widetilde X}$ is noncompact for every $w\\in W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-046.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-weighted-gs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F_d$ have the weighted basis above, and let $(r_\\lambda)$ be a finite or countable family of relators with positive integer bounds $v_\\lambda$ satisfying $$\\nu(r_\\lambda)\\ge v_\\lambda\\ge1.$$ Assume only finitely many $v_\\lambda$ lie below any fixed bound.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pinched-sphere",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi_{\\mathrm{pair}}$ be the quotient of $\\Pi$ obtained by killing the boundary loops of its 24 two-puncture disks. It is free of rank 24, and its pro-2 completion is naturally $$\\label{eq:pinched-sphere-group}\n \\widehat{\\Pi_{\\mathrm{pair}}}\n \\simeq L/\\overline{\\langle\\!\\langle\n              \\sigma(g)g^{-1}:g\\in L\n                    \\rangle\\!\\rangle}.$$ More precisely, let $\\lambda:L\\twoheadrightarrow R$ be a continuous quotient of pro-2 groups satisfying $\\lambda\\sigma=\\lambda$. There is a homomorphism $\\theta:\\Pi\\to R$ with dense image and $$\\label{eq:pinching-square}\n \\theta p=\\lambda\\Phi|_K.$$ For $m\\in\\Gamma$, the condition $$\\label{eq:torus-equalizer-condition}\n \\lambda\\Phi(\\varphi_m(k))=\\lambda\\Phi(k)\n \\qquad(k\\in K)$$ implies $\\theta\\beta_m=\\theta$ on the entire unpinched group $\\Pi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-046.2"
      },

There is a homomorphism $\\theta:\\Pi\\to R$ with dense image and $$\\label{eq:pinching-square}\n \\theta p=\\lambda\\Phi|_K.$$ For $m\\in\\Gamma$, the condition $$\\label{eq:torus-equalizer-condition}\n \\lambda\\Phi(\\varphi_m(k))=\\lambda\\Phi(k)\n \\qquad(k\\in K)$$ implies $\\theta\\beta_m=\\theta$ on the entire unpinched group $\\Pi$.",

      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pinched-sphere",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi_{\\mathrm{pair}}$ be the quotient of $\\Pi$ obtained by killing the boundary loops of its 24 two-puncture disks. It is free of rank 24, and its pro-2 completion is naturally $$\\label{eq:pinched-sphere-group}\n \\widehat{\\Pi_{\\mathrm{pair}}}\n \\simeq L/\\overline{\\langle\\!\\langle\n              \\sigma(g)g^{-1}:g\\in L\n                    \\rangle\\!\\rangle}.$$ More precisely, let $\\lambda:L\\twoheadrightarrow R$ be a continuous quotient of pro-2 groups satisfying $\\lambda\\sigma=\\lambda$. There is a homomorphism $\\theta:\\Pi\\to R$ with dense image and $$\\label{eq:pinching-square}\n \\theta p=\\lambda\\Phi|_K.$$ For $m\\in\\Gamma$, the condition $$\\label{eq:torus-equalizer-condition}\n \\lambda\\Phi(\\varphi_m(k))=\\lambda\\Phi(k)\n \\qquad(k\\in K)$$ implies $\\theta\\beta_m=\\theta$ on the entire unpinched group $\\Pi$.",
          "source_id": "PM-046.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-pinched-sphere"
    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-involution",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be the free pro-$2$ group on $a_{h,j}$, with $h\\in(\\mathbb Z/4)^2$ and $0\\le j\\le2$, and let $\\sigma(a_{h,j})=a_{b_j-h,j}^{-1}$, where $b_j\\bmod2\\ne0$. Equip $L$ with the basis and weights of [lem:shift-basis](#lem:shift-basis). There are 24 elements $g\\in L$, with assigned positive weights $w_g$, such that $$\\nu_L(g)\\ge w_g,\\qquad\n \\nu_L(r_g)\\ge w_g+1,\n \\qquad r_g=\\sigma(g)g^{-1},$$ and the closed normal subgroup generated by the $r_g$ imposes $\\sigma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$ on all of $L$. The basis polynomial and the polynomial of the chosen weights are $$\\label{eq:weight-polynomials}\n \\begin{split}\n P(t)&=\\sum_{i,\\ell,j}t^{i+\\ell+1}\n       =3t(1+t+t^2+t^3)^2,\\\\\n G(t)&=\\sum_g t^{w_g}\n       =3t(1+t)(1+t^2)^2.\n \\end{split}$$ In particular, $P=(1+t)G$, and these involution relations have degree cost at most $tG(t)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-046.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-involution",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-detection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex surface, let $D$ be a simple-normal-crossings divisor, and put $U=Y\\setminus D$. Suppose $\\rho:\\pi_1(U)\\to Q$ has dense image in a profinite group and sends every component meridian of $D$ to an element of finite order. There is a finite quotient $Q\\twoheadrightarrow P_f$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The quotient is injective on the image under $\\rho$ of every sufficiently small local boundary-complement group.*\n\n2.  *The connected finite cover $\\pi:U'\\to U$ defined by $\\ker(\\pi_1(U)\\to P_f)$ extends to a projective morphism $p:X\\to Y$, where $X$ is smooth and connected and $D_X=X\\setminus U'$ is a simple-normal-crossings divisor.*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $i:U'\\hookrightarrow X$ for inclusion, there is a homomorphism $\\rho_X:\\pi_1(X)\\to Q$ such that $$\\rho_X\\circ i_* = \\rho\\circ\\pi_*.\n        \\label{eq:rho-descent}$$*\n\n*Moreover, the resolution used to construct $X$ can be chosen to preserve every open subset of the normalization on which the surface is smooth and its reduced boundary has simple normal crossings.",
        "source_id": "PM-046.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-detection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex surface, let $D$ be a simple-normal-crossings divisor, and put $U=Y\\setminus D$. Suppose $\\rho:\\pi_1(U)\\to Q$ has dense image in a profinite group and sends every component meridian of $D$ to an element of finite order. There is a finite quotient $Q\\twoheadrightarrow P_f$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The quotient is injective on the image under $\\rho$ of every sufficiently small local boundary-complement group.*\n\n2.  *The connected finite cover $\\pi:U'\\to U$ defined by $\\ker(\\pi_1(U)\\to P_f)$ extends to a projective morphism $p:X\\to Y$, where $X$ is smooth and connected and $D_X=X\\setminus U'$ is a simple-normal-crossings divisor.*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $i:U'\\hookrightarrow X$ for inclusion, there is a homomorphism $\\rho_X:\\pi_1(X)\\to Q$ such that $$\\rho_X\\circ i_* = \\rho\\circ\\pi_*.\n        \\label{eq:rho-descent}$$*\n\n*Moreover, the resolution used to construct $X$ can be chosen to preserve every open subset of the normalization on which the surface is smooth and its reduced boundary has simple normal crossings.",
          "source_id": "PM-046.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex surface, let $D$ be a simple-normal-crossings divisor, and put $U=Y\\setminus D$. Suppose $\\rho:\\pi_1(U)\\to Q$ has dense image in a profinite group and sends every component meridian of $D$ to an element of finite order. There is a finite quotient $Q\\twoheadrightarrow P_f$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The quotient is injective on the image under $\\rho$ of every sufficiently small local boundary-complement group.*\n\n2.  *The connected finite cover $\\pi:U'\\to U$ defined by $\\ker(\\pi_1(U)\\to P_f)$ extends to a projective morphism $p:X\\to Y$, where $X$ is smooth and connected and $D_X=X\\setminus U'$ is a simple-normal-crossings divisor.*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $i:U'\\hookrightarrow X$ for inclusion, there is a homomorphism $\\rho_X:\\pi_1(X)\\to Q$ such that $$\\rho_X\\circ i_* = \\rho\\circ\\pi_*.\n        \\label{eq:rho-descent}$$*\n\n*Moreover, the resolution used to construct $X$ can be chosen to preserve every open subset of the normalization on which the surface is smooth and its reduced boundary has simple normal crossings.",
      "explanation": "A finite quotient detects entire local boundary images and permits descent across normalization and resolution.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-detection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a smooth connected projective complex surface, let $D$ be a simple-normal-crossings divisor, and put $U=Y\\setminus D$. Suppose $\\rho:\\pi_1(U)\\to Q$ has dense image in a profinite group and sends every component meridian of $D$ to an element of finite order. There is a finite quotient $Q\\twoheadrightarrow P_f$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *The quotient is injective on the image under $\\rho$ of every sufficiently small local boundary-complement group.*\n\n2.  *The connected finite cover $\\pi:U'\\to U$ defined by $\\ker(\\pi_1(U)\\to P_f)$ extends to a projective morphism $p:X\\to Y$, where $X$ is smooth and connected and $D_X=X\\setminus U'$ is a simple-normal-crossings divisor.*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $i:U'\\hookrightarrow X$ for inclusion, there is a homomorphism $\\rho_X:\\pi_1(X)\\to Q$ such that $$\\rho_X\\circ i_* = \\rho\\circ\\pi_*.\n        \\label{eq:rho-descent}$$*\n\n*Moreover, the resolution used to construct $X$ can be chosen to preserve every open subset of the normalization on which the surface is smooth and its reduced boundary has simple normal crossings.",
          "source_id": "PM-046.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Holomorphic hull** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a complex manifold $V$ and a nonempty compact set $K\subset V$, its *holomorphic hull* is $$\widehat K_V=\{v\in V: \|g(v)\|\leq\sup_K\|g\| \text{ for every }g\in\mathcal O(V)\}.$$
- **Holomorphic convexity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Holomorphic convexity means that every such hull is compact.
- **Meridian** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *meridian* is the boundary of a small disk transverse to a divisor.
- **Pro-group** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A pro-2 group is an inverse limit of finite groups of order a power of two.
- **Magnus valuation** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $g\ne1$, let $\nu(g)$ be the least degree in $g-1$, and put $\nu(1)=\infty$.
- **Frattini quotient** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The *Frattini quotient* of a finitely generated pro-2 group $G$ is $$G/\overline{G^2[G,G]},$$ an $\mathbb F_2$-vector space.
- **Translation difference** ([source](#eq:shift-basis)): For an automorphism $T$, write $\delta_T(g)=T(g)g^{-1}$, and define $$\label{eq:shift-basis} u_{i\ell,j}=\delta_{T_1}^{\,i}\delta_{T_2}^{\,\ell}(a_{0,j}), \qquad 0\le i,\ell<4.$$
- **Deep parameter filtration** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Give its four free generators ordinary Magnus degree one, and let $$D_N=\{m\in\widehat\Gamma:\nu_\Gamma(m)\ge N\}, \qquad \Gamma_N=\Gamma\cap D_N.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-infinite-special-image-2): A projective surface contains a rational nodal curve with infinite fundamental-group image, yielding a noncompact holomorphic hull in its universal cover. -> A projective surface contains a rational nodal curve with infinite fundamental-group image, yielding a noncompact holomorphic hull in its universal cover.
- **S2** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-hull) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-hull): A rational nodal curve with infinite ambient fundamental-group image obstructs holomorphic convexity through its lifted compact components. -> A rational nodal curve with infinite ambient fundamental-group image obstructs holomorphic convexity through its lifted compact components.
- **S3** [claim](#stmt-lem-substitution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-substitution): Generator valuation estimates extend to degree estimates on the completed Magnus algebra. -> Generator valuation estimates extend to degree estimates on the completed Magnus algebra.
- **S4** [claim](#stmt-lem-weighted-gs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weighted-gs): A convergent negative weighted presentation expression forces the pro-group quotient to be infinite. -> A convergent negative weighted presentation expression forces the pro-group quotient to be infinite.
- **S5** [claim](#stmt-lem-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family): The elliptic multiplication construction gives disjoint moving markings and paired simple collisions. -> The elliptic multiplication construction gives disjoint moving markings and paired simple collisions.
- **S6** [claim](#stmt-prop-based-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-based-monodromy): The corrected torus monodromy is compatible with actual based sphere monodromy through a surjective fiber-group map. -> The corrected torus monodromy is compatible with actual based sphere monodromy through a surjective fiber-group map.
- **S7** [claim](#stmt-lem-degree-one) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-degree-one): Invariant double-cover characters detect that corrected monodromy and sign preserve the weighted first quotient. -> Invariant double-cover characters detect that corrected monodromy and sign preserve the weighted first quotient.
- **S8** [claim](#stmt-prop-monodromy-raising) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-monodromy-raising): Corrected monodromy raises weighted Magnus degree. -> Corrected monodromy raises weighted Magnus degree.
- **S9** [claim](#stmt-lem-pinched-sphere) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pinched-sphere): Collapsing pair boundaries produces the sign quotient and transfers monodromy equalities to the unpinched sphere group. -> Collapsing pair boundaries produces the sign quotient and transfers monodromy equalities to the unpinched sphere group.
- **S10** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-geometric-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-geometric-monodromy): The finite geometric action allows a parameter cover preserving markings and eliminating sign. -> The finite geometric action allows a parameter cover preserving markings and eliminating sign.
- **S11** [claim](#stmt-lem-shift-basis) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-shift-basis): Translation differences give a free weighted basis with degree-raising shifts and sign action. -> Translation differences give a free weighted basis with degree-raising shifts and sign action.
- **S12** [claim](#stmt-prop-involution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-involution): An economical selection of involution relators imposes the full sign action while preserving the weighted margin. -> An economical selection of involution relators imposes the full sign action while preserving the weighted margin.
- **S13** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer): The semidirect-product substitution transfers valuation lower bounds to the fiber quotient. -> The semidirect-product substitution transfers valuation lower bounds to the fiber quotient.
- **S14** [claim](#stmt-lem-deep-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deep-invariance): Basis monodromy equalities extend to the deep parameter subgroup and then to the actual sphere fiber. -> Basis monodromy equalities extend to the deep parameter subgroup and then to the actual sphere fiber.
- **S15** [claim](#stmt-lem-distinguished-meridians) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-distinguished-meridians): Distinguished vertical boundary meridians are killed by the section splitting and pair pinching. -> Distinguished vertical boundary meridians are killed by the section splitting and pair pinching.
- **S16** [claim](#stmt-prop-infinite-quotient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-infinite-quotient): The quotient remains infinite after boundary powers are imposed, while local meridians have finite image. -> The quotient remains infinite after boundary powers are imposed, while local meridians have finite image.
- **S17** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-detection): A finite quotient detects entire local boundary images and permits descent across normalization and resolution. -> A finite quotient detects entire local boundary images and permits descent across normalization and resolution.
- **S18** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-special-fiber) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-special-fiber): The distinguished reduced fiber remains a nodal union of smooth rational components. -> The distinguished reduced fiber remains a nodal union of smooth rational components.
- **S19** [claim](#stmt-lem-infinite-special-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-infinite-special-image): A neighborhood retraction transfers the infinite nearby-fiber image to a connected component of the compact rational fiber. -> A neighborhood retraction transfers the infinite nearby-fiber image to a connected component of the compact rational fiber.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **weighted_margin**: The basis and selected involution weights leave a strictly negative weighted expression after the reserved relation costs.
- **finite_complement**: The chosen homogeneous complement and its differential image span the truncated Frattini algebra.
- **local_descent**: Detecting entire finite local boundary-group images permits descent across normalization and resolution.
- **rational_hull**: An infinite fundamental-group image of the rational nodal curve forces noncompact holomorphic hulls in the universal cover.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **finite_check_as_surface_proof**: Treat the finite weight and complement calculation as a certificate of the constructed surface. -> The calculation does not establish the monodromy relations, descent or infinite curve image.
- **cyclic_detection_only**: Detect each boundary meridian separately and assume descent across a crossing. -> Relations between commuting meridians can escape separate cyclic detection.
- **rational_components_only**: Infer a noncompact holomorphic hull merely from the presence of rational components. -> The connected curve need not have infinite image in the ambient fundamental group.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The homogeneous complement and weight-polynomial calculation are finite constructive parts of the involution proof. This check does not certify the infinite quotient or the projective surface.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON pair [b1,b2], each an integer residue modulo four.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each residue lies in range(4); at least one is odd.
# OUTPUTS: Exact complement ranks and exact weight values on the bounded rational grid, or a precondition error.
# TIME: Bounded polynomial arithmetic and elimination on sixteen coordinates; bounded rational grid.
# SPACE: Bounded lists of sixteen polynomial coefficients and grid results.
# ARITHMETIC: GF(2) polynomial coefficients and certified exact flint fmpq arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid JSON or violated residue preconditions returns an error object.
import sys, json
from flint import fmpq

def multiply(p, q):
    z = [0] * 16
    for i, a in enumerate(p):
        for j, b in enumerate(q):
            x, y = i//4+j//4, i%4+j%4
            if a and b and x < 4 and y < 4:
                z[4*x+y] ^= 1
    return z

def power(p, k):
    z = [1] + [0]*15
    for _ in range(k):
        z = multiply(z, p)
    return z

def add(p, q):
    return [x ^ y for x, y in zip(p, q)]

def rank(columns):
    pivots = {}
    for col in columns:
        z = sum(a << i for i, a in enumerate(col))
        while z:
            p = z.bit_length()-1
            if p in pivots:
                z ^= pivots[p]
            else:
                pivots[p] = z
                break
    return len(pivots)

def run(pair):
    if not isinstance(pair, list) or len(pair) != 2 or any(type(x) != int or not 0 <= x < 4 for x in pair):
        return {'error':'residue precondition'}
    b1,b2 = pair
    if not (b1%2 or b2%2):
        return {'error':'odd displacement required'}
    xi = [0]*16; xi[4] = 1
    eta = [0]*16; eta[1] = 1
    one = [1]+[0]*15
    u = add(xi if b1%2 else [0]*16, eta if b2%2 else [0]*16)
    v = eta if b1%2 else xi
    C = [multiply(power(u,a),power(v,b)) for a in (0,2) for b in range(4)]
    invx = add(add(xi,power(xi,2)),power(xi,3))
    invy = add(add(eta,power(eta,2)),power(eta,3))
    prefactor = multiply(power(add(one,xi),b1),power(add(one,eta),b2))
    def sigma(poly):
        s = [0]*16
        for k,coef in enumerate(poly):
            if coef:
                s = add(s,multiply(power(invx,k//4),power(invy,k%4)))
        return multiply(prefactor,s)
    rels = [add(sigma(c),c) for c in C]
    values=[]
    for numerator in range(51):
        t=fmpq(numerator,200)
        P=sum((t**(i+j+1) for i in range(4) for j in range(4) for block in range(3)),fmpq(0))
        G=sum((t**(a+b+1) for a in (0,2) for b in range(4) for block in range(3)),fmpq(0))
        deficit=1-P+t*G+fmpq(1,50)
        eligible=0 < t < fmpq(1,4)
        values.append([str(P),str(G),str(deficit),bool(eligible and deficit < -fmpq(1,125))])
    return {'ranks':[rank(C),rank(rels),rank(C+rels)],'values':values}
try:
    answer=run(json.loads(sys.stdin.read()))
except (ValueError,TypeError):
    answer={'error':'residue precondition'}
print(json.dumps(answer,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')))
```

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON pair [b1,b2], each an integer residue modulo four.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Each residue lies in range(4); at least one is odd.\n# OUTPUTS: Exact complement ranks and exact weight values on the bounded rational grid, or a precondition error.\n# TIME: Bounded polynomial arithmetic and elimination on sixteen coordinates; bounded rational grid.\n# SPACE: Bounded lists of sixteen polynomial coefficients and grid results.\n# ARITHMETIC: GF(2) polynomial coefficients and certified exact flint fmpq arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid JSON or violated residue preconditions returns an error object.\nimport sys, json\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndef multiply(p, q):\n    z = [0] * 16\n    for i, a in enumerate(p):\n        for j, b in enumerate(q):\n            x, y = i//4+j//4, i%4+j%4\n            if a and b and x < 4 and y < 4:\n                z[4*x+y] ^= 1\n    return z\n\ndef power(p, k):\n    z = [1] + [0]*15\n    for _ in range(k):\n        z = multiply(z, p)\n    return z\n\ndef add(p, q):\n    return [x ^ y for x, y in zip(p, q)]\n\ndef rank(columns):\n    pivots = {}\n    for col in columns:\n        z = sum(a << i for i, a in enumerate(col))\n        while z:\n            p = z.bit_length()-1\n            if p in pivots:\n                z ^= pivots[p]\n            else:\n                pivots[p] = z\n                break\n    return len(pivots)\n\ndef run(pair):\n    if not isinstance(pair, list) or len(pair) != 2 or any(type(x) != int or not 0 <= x < 4 for x in pair):\n        return {'error':'residue precondition'}\n    b1,b2 = pair\n    if not (b1%2 or b2%2):\n        return {'error':'odd displacement required'}\n    xi = [0]*16; xi[4] = 1\n    eta = [0]*16; eta[1] = 1\n    one = [1]+[0]*15\n    u = add(xi if b1%2 else [0]*16, eta if b2%2 else [0]*16)\n    v = eta if b1%2 else xi\n    C = [multiply(power(u,a),power(v,b)) for a in (0,2) for b in range(4)]\n    invx = add(add(xi,power(xi,2)),power(xi,3))\n    invy = add(add(eta,power(eta,2)),power(eta,3))\n    prefactor = multiply(power(add(one,xi),b1),power(add(one,eta),b2))\n    def sigma(poly):\n        s = [0]*16\n        for k,coef in enumerate(poly):\n            if coef:\n                s = add(s,multiply(power(invx,k//4),power(invy,k%4)))\n        return multiply(prefactor,s)\n    rels = [add(sigma(c),c) for c in C]\n    values=[]\n    for numerator in range(51):\n        t=fmpq(numerator,200)\n        P=sum((t**(i+j+1) for i in range(4) for j in range(4) for block in range(3)),fmpq(0))\n        G=sum((t**(a+b+1) for a in (0,2) for b in range(4) for block in range(3)),fmpq(0))\n        deficit=1-P+t*G+fmpq(1,50)\n        eligible=0 < t < fmpq(1,4)\n        values.append([str(P),str(G),str(deficit),bool(eligible and deficit < -fmpq(1,125))])\n    return {'ranks':[rank(C),rank(rels),rank(C+rels)],'values':values}\ntry:\n    answer=run(json.loads(sys.stdin.read()))\nexcept (ValueError,TypeError):\n    answer={'error':'residue precondition'}\nprint(json.dumps(answer,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')))\n",

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Exact symbolic software can adapt the finite weight-polynomial calculation to check the stated involution relation budget. This is a check of an algebraic input to the construction; a general software correctness contract is ABSENT. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The moving punctures and rational fiber are geometric inputs to a proof. A game model, simulation update rule and numerical stability contract are ABSENT. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A symbolic algebra tool can adapt the finite Frattini-quotient calculation to check the displayed differential and complementary subspace. A tool that constructs the full geometric quotient is ABSENT. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The proof specifies a finite monomial selection and differential calculation that can be adapted into an exact algorithm for choosing the linear data behind the involution relations. Construction of all geometric and deep monodromy relations is not supplied as a finite algorithm. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The dual graph gives a finite representation of the incidence topology of the connected nodal rational fiber. Its use is conditional on the components being simply connected and the crossings having the stated nodal structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted algebra argument and surface construction provide no accelerator architecture, memory layout or performance contract. Hardware applicability is ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction produces an infinite pro-2 quotient for a geometric obstruction. A cryptographic primitive, adversarial model or computational hardness assertion is ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The holomorphic-convexity obstruction contains no learning objective, statistical model or training guarantee. Machine-learning applicability is ABSENT. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The strict weighted inequality is a sufficient condition for group infinitude. An optimisation objective, feasible scheduling model and optimality guarantee are ABSENT. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite local boundary-group detection supports descent across a geometric compactification. A distributed protocol, failure model and agreement guarantee are ABSENT. |
| other (Complex projective geometry and geometric topology) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The stated result applies directly to complex projective geometry: an infinite fundamental-group image of a connected nodal curve with smooth rational components obstructs holomorphic convexity of the universal cover. A finite procedure constructing or certifying the whole surface is ABSENT. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample concerns the ordinary simply connected universal cover of the constructed smooth connected projective complex surface. Blocked use: ['Do not assert that every projective surface has a nonconvex universal cover.', 'Do not extend the conclusion to arbitrary reductive covers or partial compactifications.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The rational-curve obstruction requires a compact connected nodal curve with smooth rational irreducible components and infinite image in the ambient fundamental group. Blocked use: ['Do not infer noncompact holomorphic hulls from rational components alone.', 'Do not omit connectedness, compactness, nodality or the infinite-image hypothesis.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): Weighted infinitude requires lower bounds on all relator degrees, convergence at the chosen parameter and only finitely many bounds below any fixed bound. Blocked use: ['Do not certify infinitude from the generator and involution polynomials alone.', 'Do not treat a finite relator sample as the complete countable presentation.']
- **L4** (edge_case): The monodromy relation-cost formula is used only in its strict convergence regime. Blocked use: ['Do not evaluate the geometric-series formula at or beyond its convergence threshold.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The economical involution presentation assumes the displacement has nonzero reduction modulo the stated subgroup and uses the adapted basis and weights. Blocked use: ['Do not transfer the relation count and degree costs to an arbitrary involution or an arbitrary basis.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): Monodromy invariance on the geometric sphere group depends on the actual based surjection and its exact compatibility with the torus action. Blocked use: ['Do not replace based monodromy compatibility by agreement only up to an unspecified inner automorphism.', 'Do not assume the pair-pinching kernel is monodromy invariant.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): Finite local detection requires the full local boundary-group images, rather than separate tests on each cyclic meridian subgroup. Blocked use: ['Do not descend through compactification using only individual meridian detection.', 'Do not omit exceptional boundary meridians introduced by resolution.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The local-detection lemma assumes a smooth connected projective complex surface, a simple-normal-crossings divisor, a dense profinite image and finite-order component meridian images. Blocked use: ['Do not apply the descent conclusion to arbitrary singular boundaries or infinite local meridian images.']
- **L9** (nonconstructive): The parameter depth is chosen sufficiently large and may be enlarged to kill the finite geometric action; a concrete selected depth is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Do not claim a fully instantiated finite parameter cover from the numerical margin alone.']
- **L10** (nonconstructive): Boundary powers are selected only after the finite cover and compactification fix the boundary components; explicit selected boundary count and exponent are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Do not choose the boundary budget before fixing the compactified family.', 'Do not publish an invented boundary count or selected exponent.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): Finite detection uses separation of nonidentity local elements by open normal subgroups; an explicit selected finite quotient is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Do not present the abstract finite-quotient existence argument as an implemented quotient-construction algorithm.']
- **L12** (formal_scope): Exact finite polynomial or Frattini calculations check algebraic ingredients of the proof; they do not certify the infinite quotient, geometric compactification or analytic universal-cover conclusion. Blocked use: ['Do not promote a finite arithmetic certificate to verification of the complete theorem.', 'Do not interpret numerical approximation as a proof of the strict weighted inequality.']
- **L13** (hypothesis): The dual graph computes the fundamental group of the nodal union only under the stated simply connected component hypothesis; the ambient inclusion image remains a separate issue. Blocked use: ['Do not infer infinite ambient fundamental-group image merely from cycles in a finite incidence graph.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a smooth connected projective complex surface whose universal cover is not holomorphically convex. This disproves the Shafarevich conjecture on holomorphic convexity in complex dimension two.
